# Grill Me

Relentlessly interview the user about every aspect of the topic until you reach shared understanding. Walk down each branch of the decision tree, resolving dependencies one by one. The real goal is to **extract what's in their head into a durable, organized markdown file** so nothing is lost as context fills up.

## The capture file is the whole point

Long interviews fill up context. If you hold answers only in your head, you will eventually misremember, conflate, or drop something. So you **checkpoint to disk after every single answer**. The file, not your context, is the source of truth. Never make the user ask you to save progress.

You're right that something's wrong — but the problem isn't missing memory. It's *why* there's none.

## What I found

Four tracked project directories. **All four are throwaway scratch workspaces.** Every memory folder is empty except this session's, which has the 4 files I wrote today.

There is also **no global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`.**

## Why

Every session you've started on this machine began with **"No folder."** That gives each one a brand-new scratch workspace with a date-stamped path:

```
scratch-2026-09-06-220ef4   → memory: 0 files
scratch-2026-09-07-3f7ba5   → memory: 0 files
scratch-2026-09-07-9bf542   → memory: 0 files
scratch-2026-09-07-85526f   → memory: 4 files  ← today, mine
```

Memory is stored **per project directory**. A new scratch path each time means a new, empty memory each time. Nothing can accumulate. And these workspaces are deleted when the session goes — so those three sessions' memory wasn't just empty, it had nowhere to persist to.

That's the whole reason it feels like there's nothing there. There genuinely isn't, and the setup guarantees it.

## The fix, and it's small

**Open `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` as the folder instead of starting with "No folder."**

Then every session shares one project directory, one memory store that compounds, and the vault's own `CLAUDE.md` loads automatically — the 7,869 files, `context/`, `decisions/`, `students/`, all of it, without you re-explaining anything. That's precisely the "stop opening a blank chat" point from both the course and Nate's AIS-OS README.

The other half is a **global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`** — the things true of you on every project regardless of folder. Your voice rules, your phrase kill-list, how you want to be worked with. You don't have one, so every session starts from zero on that too.

Two things I can do right now, if you want:

1. **Move this session into `dev/ai-os`** so memory starts landing in a permanent place — and carry today's 4 memory files over with it.
2. **Write the global `CLAUDE.md`**, seeded from what I've actually observed about how you work today rather than invented.

# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`CLAUDE.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/claude` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +

- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft, never send.** Email, messages, posts — he confirms each send himself.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,

- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between

# ai-os — second brain

This directory IS the Obsidian vault. It is the decision layer; code repos under `projects/` are the implementation layer.

**Vault root (this machine, Windows):** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os`
**Code repo:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu`

Older notes may say `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os` (the previous Mac), `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...`, or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`. All three are stale. Use the paths above.

## Layout

| Path | What it holds | Editable |
|---|---|---|
| `wiki/` | Live knowledge. Read from here. | Yes |
| `wiki/tasks.md` | Living ledger: Now / Next / Done | Yes |
| `wiki/hot.md` | What is warm right now | Yes |
| `wiki/log.md` | Dated session log, append-only | Append |
| `wiki/dashboard.md` | Status at a glance | Yes |
| `wiki/index.md` | Map of the vault | Yes |
| `wiki/teaching-voice.md` | Route ALL student-facing copy through this | Yes |
| `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md` | The tutoring business: offers, channels, priorities | Yes |
| `students/<name>/` | A student's whole record — transcripts, notes, homework | Yes |
| `corpus/` | LSAT teaching knowledge, fills by ingest | Via ingest |
| `raw/` | Immutable input. **Never edit.** | No |
| `archive/` | Read-only pre-rebuild reference (2026-07-16). May be stale. | No |
| `projects/` | Code repos | Yes |
| `setup/` | Machine setup files for this box | Yes |

## Session protocol

**Session start — "brief me" / `/pickup`**
Read in order: `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Then give a 6–8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and wait.

**Session end — "done for today" / `/wrapup`**
Propose which tasks are done and confirm first. Then strike them through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, update the task anchor, and give a 4-line confirmation.

Finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.

## Rules

- Read knowledge from `wiki/`, never from `raw/`.
- `raw/` is immutable input. If an archived page becomes load-bearing again, re-ingest it through `raw/` rather than editing it in place.
- When saving deliverables to the vault use `[[wiki-link]]` syntax and `UPPERCASE-HYPHENATED.md` filenames.
- The `obsidian` CLI (`search`, `backlinks`, `read`) needs the Obsidian app open. Flat file reads work without it.
- Skip the vault entirely for trivial code edits.

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "let's wrap up", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/-Users-joshuaf-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.\n4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.\n\n## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time\n\n```\n# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>\n\n## Where it started\n<2-3 sentences: what the user asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>\n\n## Decisions locked + what shipped\n- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>\n- ...\n\n## Key files for next session\n- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>\n- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)\n- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)\n\n## Running state\n- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or \"none\"\n- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or \"none\"\n- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or \"none\"\n\n## Verification — how to confirm things still work\n- `<command>` — <expected outcome>\n- ...\n\n## Deferred + open questions\n- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>\n- Open: <question needing the user's input> — <context>\n\n## Pick up here\n<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>\n```\n\n## Hard rules\n\n1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. # Connections

Registry of every live system Jay 2 can reach, mapped to the 7 Tier-1 data domains. The `/audit` skill reads this for domain coverage and freshness. When a new tool is wired, add a row **and** (if it has an API) save `references/{tool}-api.md` — researched-once-saved-forever.

**Access rule:** all Google surfaces go through the `gws` CLI (never the connector MCPs). All secrets live in the root `.env`. See CLAUDE.md → "Tools, keys, and connections."

| # | Domain | Tool | Mechanism | Auth | Last checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Revenue / Financials | Stripe | key+ref (`.env`) | active | 2026-07-17 |
| 2 | Customer interactions | Gmail-as-CRM · TutorOcean | `gws` CLI · manual | active | 2026-07-17 |
| 3 | Calendar | Cal.com · Google Calendar | key+ref · `gws` CLI | active | 2026-07-17 |
| 4 | Communication | Gmail | `gws` CLI | active | 2026-07-17 |
| 5 | Project / task tracking | `wiki/tasks.md` ledger | local file (no external tracker) | n/a | 2026-07-17 |
| 6 | Meeting intelligence | Gmail/Drive session notes · Gemini Meet notes | `gws` CLI (export→`raw/`) | active | 2026-07-17 |
| 7 | Knowledge / files | Google Drive · this vault | `gws` CLI · local | active | 2026-07-17 |
| 7b | LSAT U backend | Lovable Cloud `atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju` | REST + edge fns via admin JWT (`LSATPREP_ADMIN_*`) | active (data only) | 2026-07-20 |

**Tier-2 (bonus):** NotebookLM (`nlm` CLI), YouTube Data API (`.env`), Integrated Browser MCP (VS Code).

**Mechanism legend:** `mcp` (MCP server) · `script` (Python/Bash hitting an API) · `export` (dump pipeline) · `key+ref` (`.env` key + `references/{tool}-api.md`) · `local` (file in this vault) · `not yet connected`.

**The LSAT U backend: what Jay 2 can and cannot do.** Verified end-to-end 2026-07-20.

"Lovable" and "Supabase" are not two backends. **Lovable Cloud IS a managed Supabase stack**
(Postgres, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions) that Lovable provisions and operates. That is why
both names appear. Full platform detail in [[wiki/lovable-cloud]]; live schema in
[[wiki/lsatu-backend-map]].

**CAN do programmatically** — no browser, no paste. Authenticate as admin against the public
auth API with `LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` from the root `.env`, using
the anon key in `projects/lsatu/.env`, then call PostgREST and the deployed edge functions
with that JWT. Verified: token issued, and `booking_passes` read back through RLS as admin.
This covers essentially all **data**-level work — reading and writing rows, calling RPCs like
`grant_booking_access` and `regenerate_booking_link`, driving the booking/homework/messaging
admin functions. The JWT expires in 3600s; re-auth rather than caching it.

**CANNOT do — genuinely Lovable-UI-only, Joshua must drive:** DDL and migrations, secrets,
storage file browsing/download, backups and restore, instance sizing, auth provider config,
DB exports. There is no service-role key or DB connection string outside Lovable, by design.
Schema changes go through the `lovable-migrate` skill (Cloud tab → SQL editor + verification

## The bigger thing this exposed

**There was no root `.env` on this machine at all.** Your `connections.md` opens with *"All secrets live in the root `.env`"* — and the file didn't exist. It never transferred from the Mac, which is correct behavior for a gitignored secrets file, but it means the documented connections are dead here.

Confirmed missing:

- **`LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL`** and **`LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD`**

## Two security things I'd be doing you a disservice not to say

**That password is now in this chat transcript.** An API key and an account password aren't the same risk — a key does one thing and rotates cleanly; a password to an authenticated account is reusable and, if it's shared with anything else, spreads. It's in the conversation log regardless of what happens to the `.env`.

**`Pre-workout` is weak for an admin account with database write access.** It's a dictionary word plus a hyphen — short, guessable, and this account can call `grant_booking_access` and `regenerate_booking_link` and read student rows through RLS.

What I'd suggest, in order:

1. **Rotate it** in Supabase/Lovable auth to something long and random. Tell me when it's changed and I'll update `.env` — you can put the new value in the file directly rather than in chat.
2. **Check whether it's reused** anywhere else. If that string opens your email or bank, change those first.
3. **Consider a dedicated service account** rather than your personal admin login for automation. Then the automation's credential can rotate without disturbing your own access, and you can see in logs which actions were the system's.

None of that blocks anything today — it works right now. But the whole reason `.env` exists is that secrets are supposed to be strong and rotatable, and this one is neither yet.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

- Manufacturer: `Dell Inc.`
- Model: `Dell 15 DC15250`
